"""
mesh_benchmark.py — High-speed latency benchmark for Mesh (ONNX + SQLite WAL + SIMD engine).
Run:
    python mesh_benchmark.py
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import statistics
import sys
import time
import uuid

# Ensure UTF-8 console output on Windows so box-drawing characters and emojis never fail
if hasattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure"):
    try:
        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        sys.stderr.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except Exception:
        pass

os.environ.setdefault("MESH_EMBED_BACKEND", "auto")


def bench(label: str, fn, reps: int = 5):
    times = []
    result = None
    for _ in range(reps):
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        result = fn()
        times.append((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000.0)
    avg = statistics.mean(times)
    mn = min(times)
    mx = max(times)
    p50 = statistics.median(times)
    verdict = "🟢 FAST" if avg < 50.0 else ("🟡 OK" if avg < 300.0 else "🔴 SLOW")
    print(f"  {label:<40} avg={avg:7.2f}ms  min={mn:6.2f}  max={mx:6.2f}  p50={p50:6.2f}  {verdict}")
    return avg, result


def get_id(r) -> str:
    """Extract memory_id whether result is a dict or string."""
    if isinstance(r, dict):
        return r.get("memory_id") or r.get("id") or ""
    return str(r)


def main() -> None:
    from mesh.context_builder import build_context
    from mesh.memory import (
        Mesh,
        _encode_texts,
        ensure_model_downloaded,
        get_engine_status,
        warmup_model,
    )
    from mesh.namespaces import delete_namespace
    from mesh.version import VERSION

    NS = f"bench-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print(f"  MESH v{VERSION} MCP TOOL BENCHMARK  (Auto ONNX / SIMD Hybrid Engine)")
    print("=" * 78)
    print(f"\n  Namespace : {NS}")
    print("  Reps/op   : 5\n")

    try:
        # ── Warmup ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("[ WARMUP ] Initializing embedding engine & vector cache...")
        # Wait briefly if a first-time background ONNX download is finishing
        ensure_model_downloaded(blocking=False, interactive_prompt=False)
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        warmup_model(namespace=NS, blocking=False, assume_yes=False)
        warmup_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000.0
        st = get_engine_status()
        print(
            f"           Done in {warmup_ms:.2f}ms  "
            f"backend={st['backend']}  onnx_cached={st.get('onnx_cached_on_disk')}\n"
        )

        mesh = Mesh(namespace=NS, agent_id="bench-agent")

        # ── Seed 10 memories ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("[ SEEDING ] Writing 10 base memories...")
        seed_ids: list[str] = []
        t_seed = time.perf_counter()
        for i in range(10):
            r = mesh.learn(
                content=f"Seed fact #{i}: The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. Index={i}.",
                memory_type="fact",
                tags=["bench", f"seed-{i}"],
                confidence=0.9,
            )
            seed_ids.append(get_id(r))
        seed_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t_seed) * 1000.0
        print(f"            Done. {len(seed_ids)} memories seeded in {seed_ms:.2f}ms.\n")

        print("─" * 78)
        print("  OPERATION LATENCIES  (5 reps each)")
        print("─" * 78 + "\n")

        # ── 1. learn ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("[ 1 ] mesh_learn — store a new memory (write + embed + dedup check)")
        learn_ids: list[str] = []
        learn_ctr = [0]

        def do_learn():
            learn_ctr[0] += 1
            r = mesh.learn(
                content=(
                    f"AI agents use shared memory to coordinate efficiently. "
                    f"seq={learn_ctr[0]} ts={time.perf_counter():.6f}"
                ),
                memory_type="fact",
                tags=["bench"],
                confidence=0.85,
            )
            learn_ids.append(get_id(r))
            return r

        bench("learn (write + embed + dedup check)", do_learn)
        bench(
            "learn (duplicate in-place reinforcement)",
            lambda: mesh.learn(
                content="Seed fact #0: The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. Index=0.",
                memory_type="fact",
                tags=["bench", "reinforced"],
                confidence=0.95,
            ),
        )

        # ── 2. recall ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 2 ] mesh_recall — 3-stage hybrid semantic + BM25 + token search")
        bench(
            "recall top-5  ('AI agents coordinate')",
            lambda: mesh.recall("AI agents coordinate using memory", n=5),
        )
        bench(
            "recall top-1  ('quick brown fox')",
            lambda: mesh.recall("quick brown fox", n=1),
        )
        bench(
            "recall top-10 ('benchmark seed')",
            lambda: mesh.recall("benchmark seed memory index", n=10),
        )

        # ── 3. update ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 3 ] mesh_update — modify existing memory in-place (re-embed)")
        target_id = seed_ids[0]
        upd_ctr = [0]

        def do_update():
            upd_ctr[0] += 1
            return mesh.update(
                memory_id=target_id,
                content=(
                    f"Updated: Mesh is a high-speed shared memory layer. rev={upd_ctr[0]} ts={time.perf_counter():.6f}"
                ),
                confidence=0.95,
            )

        bench("update (re-embed + overwrite)", do_update)

        # ── 4. forget ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 4 ] mesh_forget — delete memory by ID")
        forget_pool = [x for x in learn_ids if x]

        def do_forget():
            if forget_pool:
                mesh.forget(memory_id=forget_pool.pop())
            else:
                r = mesh.learn(content=f"temp ts={time.perf_counter()}", memory_type="fact")
                mesh.forget(memory_id=get_id(r))

        bench("forget (delete by ID)", do_forget)

        # ── 5. get / inspect ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 5 ] mesh_inspect — fetch single memory by ID & list recent")
        bench("get (fetch single memory by ID)", lambda: mesh.get(memory_id=seed_ids[-1]))
        bench("inspect (list top-10 memories)", lambda: mesh.inspect(limit=10))

        # ── 6. count ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 6 ] mesh_count — count all memories in namespace")
        bench("count (namespace total)", lambda: mesh.count())

        # ── 7. context ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 7 ] mesh_context — build system prompt string")
        bench(
            "build_context top-20 (general)",
            lambda: build_context(namespace=NS, max_memories=20),
        )
        bench(
            "build_context top-5  (focused query)",
            lambda: build_context(
                query="AI agents coordinate",
                namespace=NS,
                max_memories=5,
            ),
        )

        # ── 8. patterns ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 8 ] mesh_patterns — detect topic patterns & gaps")
        bench("patterns (detect)", lambda: mesh.patterns())

        # ── 9. contradictions ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 9 ] mesh_contradictions — scan for conflicting facts")
        bench("contradictions (detect)", lambda: mesh.contradictions())

        # ── 10. embed cache hit vs miss ────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 10 ] embed cache — uncached inference vs LRU cache hit")
        bench(
            "embed MISS (uncached phrase inference)",
            lambda: _encode_texts([f"unique uncached phrase ever {uuid.uuid4()}"]),
        )
        repeated_phrase = "the shared mesh benchmark repeated text for cache hit"
        _encode_texts([repeated_phrase])
        bench(
            "embed HIT  (LRU cache, zero inference)",
            lambda: _encode_texts([repeated_phrase]),
        )

        # ── 11. project_brief ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 11 ] mesh_project_brief — structured mission, open tasks, recent completions & facts")
        bench("project_brief (budget 1500 tokens)", lambda: mesh.project_brief(max_tokens=1500))
        bench("project_brief (fast 500 tokens)", lambda: mesh.project_brief(max_tokens=500))

        # ── 12. work items: create ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 12 ] mesh_task_create — create goal and granular tasks")
        created_tasks: list[dict] = []
        task_ctr = [0]

        def do_create_task():
            task_ctr[0] += 1
            t = mesh.create_work_item(
                title=f"Task #{task_ctr[0]}: Implement high-performance worker",
                description="Worker thread pool with bounded queue",
                kind="task",
                priority="high",
                tags=["infra", "perf"],
            )
            created_tasks.append(t)
            return t

        bench("create_work_item (task)", do_create_task)

        # ── 13. work items: list ───────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 13 ] mesh_task_list — query open tasks with filtering")
        bench("list_work_items (status=todo)", lambda: mesh.list_work_items(status="todo", limit=50))

        # ── 14. work items: update ─────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 14 ] mesh_task_update — update work item status and metadata")
        target_task_id = created_tasks[0]["id"] if created_tasks else "task-1"
        bench(
            "update_work_item (in_progress)",
            lambda: mesh.update_work_item(
                item_id=target_task_id,
                status="in_progress",
                note="Worker thread implementation underway",
            ),
        )

        # ── 15. work items: complete ───────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 15 ] mesh_task_complete — complete work item with summary & evidence")
        bench(
            "complete_work_item (done + event logging)",
            lambda: mesh.complete_work_item(
                item_id=target_task_id,
                summary="Implemented worker pool with thread-safe queue",
                evidence=["src/worker.py", "tests/test_worker.py"],
            ),
        )

        # ── 16. changelog ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print("\n[ 16 ] mesh_log — generate project changelog")
        bench("changelog (markdown format)", lambda: mesh.changelog(since="7d", format="markdown"))
        bench("changelog (json format)", lambda: mesh.changelog(since="7d", format="json"))

        print("\n" + "=" * 78)
        print("  ENGINE STATUS (post-benchmark)")
        print("=" * 78)
        for k, v in get_engine_status().items():
            print(f"  {k:<40} {v}")

    finally:
        # ── Cleanup ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        print(f"\n[ CLEANUP ] Deleting bench namespace '{NS}'...")
        try:
            delete_namespace(NS)
            print("            Done.")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"            Skipped: {e}")

    print("\n✅ Benchmark complete!\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

=== FILE: benchmarks/scale_benchmark.py (LINES: 422) ===
"""
benchmarks/scale_benchmark.py — Scale and capacity benchmark for Mesh.
Evaluates throughput, latency percentiles (p50, p95, p99), RAM, and database
footprint across 1,000, 5,000, and 10,000 memories.

Run:
    python benchmarks/scale_benchmark.py
    python benchmarks/scale_benchmark.py --scales 1000,5000,10000 --json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import ctypes
import json
import os
import random
import statistics
import sys
import tempfile
import time
import uuid
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Tuple

# Console UTF-8 handling on Windows
if hasattr(sys.stdout, "reconfigure"):
    try:
        sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        sys.stderr.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
    except Exception:
        pass


def get_process_ram_mb() -> float:
    """Return process resident set size (RSS) in megabytes, stdlib only."""
    if sys.platform == "win32":
        try:
            from ctypes import wintypes

            class PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS(ctypes.Structure):
                _fields_ = [
                    ("cb", wintypes.DWORD),
                    ("PageFaultCount", wintypes.DWORD),
                    ("PeakWorkingSetSize", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("WorkingSetSize", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("QuotaPeakPagedPoolUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("QuotaPagedPoolUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("QuotaPeakNonPagedPoolUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("QuotaNonPagedPoolUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("PagefileUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                    ("PeakPagefileUsage", ctypes.c_size_t),
                ]

            psapi = ctypes.WinDLL("psapi")
            kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL("kernel32")
            GetProcessMemoryInfo = psapi.GetProcessMemoryInfo
            GetProcessMemoryInfo.argtypes = [
                wintypes.HANDLE,
                ctypes.POINTER(PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS),
                wintypes.DWORD,
            ]
            GetProcessMemoryInfo.restype = wintypes.BOOL

            counters = PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS()
            counters.cb = ctypes.sizeof(PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS)
            handle = kernel32.GetCurrentProcess()
            if GetProcessMemoryInfo(handle, ctypes.byref(counters), counters.cb):
                return counters.WorkingSetSize / (1024.0 * 1024.0)
        except Exception:
            pass
        return 0.0
    else:
        try:
            import resource

            rss = resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss
            if sys.platform == "darwin":
                return rss / (1024.0 * 1024.0)
            return rss / 1024.0
        except Exception:
            return 0.0


def benchmark_latencies(label: str, fn: Callable[[], Any], reps: int = 15) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Measure latency percentiles in milliseconds across multiple runs."""
    times_ms: List[float] = []
    for _ in range(reps):
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        fn()
        elapsed_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000.0
        times_ms.append(elapsed_ms)

    times_ms.sort()
    n = len(times_ms)
    p50 = statistics.median(times_ms)
    p95 = times_ms[int(n * 0.95)] if n >= 20 else times_ms[-1]
    p99 = times_ms[int(n * 0.99)] if n >= 100 else times_ms[-1]
    mean_val = statistics.mean(times_ms)
    min_val = min(times_ms)
    max_val = max(times_ms)

    return {
        "label": label,
        "reps": reps,
        "mean_ms": round(mean_val, 3),
        "min_ms": round(min_val, 3),
        "max_ms": round(max_val, 3),
        "p50_ms": round(p50, 3),
        "p95_ms": round(p95, 3),
        "p99_ms": round(p99, 3),
    }


def generate_synthetic_corpus(count: int, seed: int = 42) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    """Generate structured synthetic memories for scale benchmarks."""
    rng = random.Random(seed)
    domains = ["database", "networking", "auth", "infra", "billing", "frontend"]
    types = ["fact", "decision", "preference", "context"]

    memories: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    for i in range(count):
        domain = rng.choice(domains)
        m_type = rng.choice(types)
        content = (
            f"Service {domain}-{i % 100} in cluster {domain} configured with "
            f"port={8000 + (i % 500)}, replica_count={1 + (i % 16)}, "
            f"timeout_seconds={30 + (i % 60)}. Hostname is {domain}-node-{i}.internal.net."
        )
        memories.append(
            {
                "id": str(uuid.uuid4()),
                "content": content,
                "memory_type": m_type,
                "confidence": round(0.7 + (rng.random() * 0.3), 2),
                "tags": [domain, f"cluster-{i % 20}", f"scale-{count}"],
                "source_agent": f"agent-{domain}",
                "local_only": False,
            }
        )
    return memories


def run_scale_test(target_scale: int, verbose: bool = True) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Run full scale benchmark for a specific memory count."""
    from mesh.audit import get_mesh_dir
    from mesh.memory import Mesh
    from mesh.namespaces import delete_namespace

    ns = f"scale-bench-{target_scale}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}"
    mesh = Mesh(namespace=ns, agent_id="scale-agent")

    initial_ram = get_process_ram_mb()
    corpus = generate_synthetic_corpus(target_scale)

    if verbose:
        print(f"\n[{target_scale:,} MEMORIES] Generating & inserting batch...")

    # 1. Batch Ingestion Throughput
    from mesh.memory import _encode_texts_with_tag

    t_ingest_start = time.perf_counter()
    chunk_size = 500
    now_iso = time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime())
    for offset in range(0, target_scale, chunk_size):
        chunk = corpus[offset : offset + chunk_size]
        texts = [m["content"] for m in chunk]
        vectors, emb_tag = _encode_texts_with_tag(texts)
        batch_items = []
        for idx, mem in enumerate(chunk):
            vec = vectors[idx].tolist()
            metadata = {
                "memory_type": mem["memory_type"],
                "confidence": mem["confidence"],
                "source_agent": mem["source_agent"],
                "tags": mem["tags"],
                "created_at": now_iso,
                "updated_at": now_iso,
                "accessed_at": now_iso,
                "access_count": 0,
                "ttl_days": None,
                "decay_start_at": None,
                "embedding_model": emb_tag,
                "trust_tier": "user",
                "quarantined": False,
                "secret_ref": None,
            }
            batch_items.append((mem["id"], mem["content"], vec, metadata, mem["local_only"]))
        mesh.store.upsert_batch(batch_items)
    t_ingest_elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t_ingest_start
    ingest_throughput = target_scale / t_ingest_elapsed if t_ingest_elapsed > 0 else 0

    # 2. Database Footprint
    db_file = Path(get_mesh_dir()) / f"mesh_{ns}.db"
    wal_file = Path(get_mesh_dir()) / f"mesh_{ns}.db-wal"
    db_size_kb = (db_file.stat().st_size if db_file.exists() else 0) / 1024.0
    wal_size_kb = (wal_file.stat().st_size if wal_file.exists() else 0) / 1024.0
    total_db_kb = db_size_kb + wal_size_kb

    # 3. RAM after loading Vector Cache
    loaded_ram = get_process_ram_mb()
    ram_delta_mb = max(0.0, loaded_ram - initial_ram)

    if verbose:
        print(
            f"  Ingested {target_scale:,} in {t_ingest_elapsed:.2f}s "
            f"({ingest_throughput:.1f} memories/sec) | DB size: {total_db_kb:.1f} KB | RAM delta: {ram_delta_mb:.2f} MB"
        )

    # 4. Populate work items for task and brief scale evaluation
    work_count = max(50, target_scale // 5)
    with mesh.store._connect() as conn:
        for i in range(work_count):
            w_id = f"wi-{i}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}"
            s_id = f"task-{i + 1}" if i % 4 != 0 else f"goal-{(i // 4) + 1}"
            kind = "goal" if i % 4 == 0 else "task"
            status = "done" if i % 3 == 0 else ("in_progress" if i % 3 == 1 else "todo")
            title = f"Scale work item #{i}: Optimize subsystem {i % 20}"
            completed_at = now_iso if status == "done" else None
            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO work_items (id, short_id, namespace, kind, parent_id, title,
                                        description, status, priority, created_by, created_at,
                                        started_at, completed_at, completed_by, summary,
                                        evidence, tags, local_only)
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    w_id,
                    s_id,
                    ns,
                    kind,
                    None,
                    title,
                    "Synthetic description for scale benchmark",
                    status,
                    "medium",
                    "scale-agent",
                    now_iso,
                    now_iso if status != "todo" else None,
                    completed_at,
                    "scale-agent" if status == "done" else None,
                    f"Summary of work #{i}" if status == "done" else None,
                    json.dumps(["file1.py", "file2.py"]) if status == "done" else json.dumps([]),
                    json.dumps(["scale", f"sub-{i % 10}"]),
                    0,
                ),
            )
            # Log an event
            conn.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO work_events (item_id, namespace, event_type, old_status,
                                         new_status, note, agent_id, timestamp)
                VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
                """,
                (
                    w_id,
                    ns,
                    "status_change",
                    None,
                    status,
                    f"Event for {s_id}",
                    "scale-agent",
                    now_iso,
                ),
            )
        conn.commit()

    # Recalculate database footprint including work_items & events
    db_size_kb = (db_file.stat().st_size if db_file.exists() else 0) / 1024.0
    wal_size_kb = (wal_file.stat().st_size if wal_file.exists() else 0) / 1024.0
    total_db_kb = db_size_kb + wal_size_kb

    # 5. Latency Benchmarks
    # A. Dense Cosine SIMD Recall
    dense_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Dense SIMD Recall (top-5)",
        lambda: mesh.recall("Service database-42 in cluster database", n=5),
        reps=20,
    )

    # B. Exact Identifier / Lexical BM25 Recall
    exact_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Exact Token BM25 Recall",
        lambda: mesh.recall("port=8420 database-node-42.internal.net", n=5),
        reps=20,
    )

    # C. Filtered Recall (Mask over all entries)
    filtered_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Filtered Recall (tags=['database'], type='fact')",
        lambda: mesh.recall(
            "Service database replica_count",
            n=5,
            tags=["database"],
            memory_type="fact",
        ),
        reps=20,
    )

    # D. Full Namespace Inspection / Pagination
    inspect_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Namespace Inspect (top-50)",
        lambda: mesh.inspect(limit=50),
        reps=20,
    )

    # E. Project Brief (combines key facts, top tasks, recent completions)
    brief_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Project Brief (budget 1000 tokens)",
        lambda: mesh.project_brief(max_tokens=1000),
        reps=15,
    )

    # F. Task List Query
    task_list_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Task List (status=todo, limit=50)",
        lambda: mesh.list_work_items(status="todo", limit=50),
        reps=20,
    )

    # G. Changelog Generation
    changelog_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Changelog Generation (markdown)",
        lambda: mesh.changelog(since="7d", format="markdown"),
        reps=15,
    )

    # H. Schema Verification / Migration Timing
    def check_schema_reopen():
        from mesh.store import MeshStore

        s = MeshStore(namespace=ns)
        s._ensure_initialized()

    schema_lat = benchmark_latencies(
        "Schema Verification Reopen",
        check_schema_reopen,
        reps=10,
    )

    # Cleanup
    try:
        delete_namespace(ns)
    except Exception:
        pass

    return {
        "scale": target_scale,
        "work_items_count": work_count,
        "ingest_time_seconds": round(t_ingest_elapsed, 3),
        "ingest_throughput_eps": round(ingest_throughput, 1),
        "db_size_kb": round(total_db_kb, 1),
        "ram_delta_mb": round(ram_delta_mb, 2),
        "latencies": {
            "dense_simd": dense_lat,
            "exact_bm25": exact_lat,
            "filtered": filtered_lat,
            "inspect": inspect_lat,
            "brief": brief_lat,
            "task_list": task_list_lat,
            "changelog": changelog_lat,
            "schema_check": schema_lat,
        },
    }


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Mesh Scale Benchmark Suite")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--scales",
        type=str,
        default="1000,5000,10000",
        help="Comma-separated memory scale tiers (e.g. 1000,5000,10000)",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--json",
        action="store_true",
        help="Output results as JSON for CI or automation",
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()

    scale_tiers = [int(s.strip()) for s in args.scales.split(",") if s.strip()]

    print("=" * 86)
    print("  MESH SCALE & LATENCY BENCHMARK SUITE (Memories + Work Items + Project Brief)")
    print("=" * 86)

    results: List[Dict[str, Any]] = []
    for tier in scale_tiers:
        res = run_scale_test(tier, verbose=not args.json)
        results.append(res)

    if args.json:
        print(json.dumps(results, indent=2))
        return

    print("\n" + "=" * 96)
    print("  SCALE BENCHMARK SUMMARY")
    print("=" * 96)
    header = f"{'Scale':<9} | {'Tasks':<7} | {'Ingest':<11} | {'DB Size':<9} | {'Dense p50':<10} | {'Brief p50':<10} | {'Tasks p50':<10} | {'Schema':<8} | {'RAM'}"
    print(header)
    print("-" * 96)

    for r in results:
        scale_str = f"{r['scale']:,}"
        tasks_str = f"{r.get('work_items_count', 0):,}"
        tp_str = f"{r['ingest_throughput_eps']:.0f} mem/s"
        db_str = f"{r['db_size_kb'] / 1024.0:.2f} MB"
        p50 = f"{r['latencies']['dense_simd']['p50_ms']:.2f} ms"
        brief_p50 = f"{r['latencies']['brief']['p50_ms']:.2f} ms"
        task_p50 = f"{r['latencies']['task_list']['p50_ms']:.2f} ms"
        schema_p50 = f"{r['latencies']['schema_check']['p50_ms']:.2f} ms"
        ram_str = f"+{r['ram_delta_mb']:.1f} MB"
        print(
            f"{scale_str:<9} | {tasks_str:<7} | {tp_str:<11} | {db_str:<9} | {p50:<10} | {brief_p50:<10} | {task_p50:<10} | {schema_p50:<8} | {ram_str}"
        )
    print("=" * 96 + "\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
=== END FILE: benchmarks/scale_benchmark.py ===
=== FILE: c_cli/mesh_fast.c (LINES: 64) ===
/*
 * c_cli/mesh_fast.c - native launcher for the Mesh CLI.
 *
 *   mesh_fast --version | -v | version     -> prints "mesh <version>" in well under 1 ms
 *   anything else                          -> exec's `python -m mesh.cli <args>` unchanged
 *
 * Why there is no daemon forwarding any more
 * ------------------------------------------
 * The previous version POSTed the bearer token to whatever was listening on the port
 * BEFORE verifying it was the Mesh daemon (the Python CLI and MCP server verify first),
 * silently truncated replies over 64 KB, pasted an unvalidated --namespace into the
 * request line, and printed raw JSON where the Python CLI prints formatted output.
 * Those are correctness and security regressions, so every non-version command now
 * goes through the real Python CLI, which already does the identity check, the flag
 * parsing and the formatting correctly.
 *
 * Build:  gcc -O2 -std=c11 -DMESH_VERSION=\"0.8.2\" c_cli/mesh_fast.c -o c_cli/mesh_fast
 *         (build_and_verify.py passes the real version from mesh/version.py)
 */
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>

#ifdef _WIN32
#include <process.h>
#else
#include <unistd.h>
#endif

#ifndef MESH_VERSION
#define MESH_VERSION "0.8.2"
#endif

static int exec_python(int argc, char **argv) {
    char **av = (char **)calloc((size_t)argc + 4, sizeof(char *));
    if (!av) return 1;
    av[1] = "-m";
    av[2] = "mesh.cli";
    for (int i = 1; i < argc; i++) av[i + 2] = argv[i];
    av[argc + 2] = NULL;
#ifdef _WIN32
    av[0] = "python";
    _execvp("python", (const char *const *)av);
    av[0] = "py";
    _execvp("py", (const char *const *)av);
#else
    av[0] = "python3";
    execvp("python3", av);
    av[0] = "python";
    execvp("python", av);
#endif
    fprintf(stderr, "mesh_fast: could not start Python (tried python3/python/py).\n");
    free(av);
    return 127;
}

int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    if (argc == 2 &&
        (strcmp(argv[1], "--version") == 0 || strcmp(argv[1], "-v") == 0 || strcmp(argv[1], "version") == 0)) {
        puts("mesh " MESH_VERSION);
        return 0;
    }
    return exec_python(argc, argv);
}
=== END FILE: c_cli/mesh_fast.c ===

# Field Test Issue Status — Post-0.8.1 Findings Addendum (v0.8.2)

**Branch:** `fix/post-0.8.1`  
**Base Version:** `0.8.1` (`a5b40ab6741f4de4b0909a5e27ac85382aa6ca59`) | **Target Version:** `0.8.2` (Beta)  
**Evaluation Scope:** Post-0.8.1 external review and probe verification findings (F1–F8).

This addendum records the resolution of findings identified after the publication of Mesh `0.8.1`. All changes are non-breaking, maintain 100% database schema backward compatibility (schema version 3), and preserve full isolated test suite stability.

---

## 1. Summary Status Table

| Finding | Severity | Subsystem | Status | Commit | Regression / Verification Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** — Claude Code MCP config path (`.mcp.json`) & legacy migration | Medium | Installer / CLI | **RESOLVED** | `fe6f512` | `tests/test_installer.py`, `tests/test_doctor_scope_precedence.py` |
| **F2** — Decision rationale tracking, frontmatter triggers, update-in-place | Low | Skill / Store / Brief | **RESOLVED** | `dcf8027` | `tests/test_work_items.py`, `scripts/check_skill.py` |
| **F3** — Cryptographic docs alignment & field-test provenance | Low | Documentation / Linter | **RESOLVED** | `aa08b20` | `scripts/check_docs.py` (`check_no_fernet_references`) |
| **F5** — Restricted publish workflow trigger (release event only) | Medium | CI / Release Security | **RESOLVED** | `5fc0d57` | Workflow syntax & dispatch inspection |
| **F6** — Code ownership and repository hygiene | Low | Governance / Hygiene | **RESOLVED** | `609c3ac` | `.github/CODEOWNERS` verified; scratch tree deleted |
| **F7** — Redundant CI test workflow removed | Low | CI / Automation | **RESOLVED** | `c750590` | `git rm .github/workflows/test.yml` |
| **F8** — Hook contract, silent brief, credential idempotence, lock retry | Medium | Security / CLI / Hooks | **RESOLVED** | `b69439b` | `tests/test_secrets_corpus.py` (8 tests), `tests/test_installer.py` |

---

## 2. F1 — Claude Code MCP Configuration Path & Legacy Migration

- **Finding:** In 0.8.1, `ClaudeCodeAdapter` configured project-scoped MCP servers in `.claude/mcp.json`. Claude Code's official project configuration standard requires repository-root `.mcp.json`. Additionally, CLI aliases `claude` and `gemini` were unmapped, and unverified agent adapters lacked visual distinction.
- **Root Cause:** Adapter path logic placed all Claude configurations under the hidden `.claude/` directory rather than separating user scope (`~/.claude.json`) from project scope (`.mcp.json`).
- **Resolution (`fe6f512`):**
  1. Updated `ClaudeCodeAdapter.get_mcp_config_path` to return `project_dir / ".mcp.json"` for project scope, and `Path.home() / ".claude.json"` for user scope.
  2. Implemented automatic detection and migration of legacy `.claude/mcp.json` configs with prompt to the user and backup creation.
  3. Added `_AGENT_ALIAS_MAP` in `mesh/installer.py` supporting `claude` -> `claude-code` and `gemini` -> `gemini-cli`.
  4. Added `verified` flag on adapters; unverified agents display `(unverified)` in `mesh install --dry-run` and `mesh doctor`.
  5. Added trust notification note when generating project-scope `.mcp.json` files for Claude Code.
- **Test Coverage:** `tests/test_installer.py` (`test_claude_code_paths`, `test_claude_code_legacy_migration`, `test_agent_alias_resolution`), `tests/test_doctor_scope_precedence.py` (all 27 tests passing).

---

## 3. F2 — Decision Rationale Tracking & Presentation Flagging

- **Finding:** Automated probe followups indicated agent decision records often omitted rationale, reducing context fidelity for collaborating agents. Also, skill frontmatter triggers and port update-in-place instructions required clarification.
- **Root Cause:** Decision capture rules did not strictly mandate causal reason markers (`because`, `due to`, `in order to`), and `rules_block.md` lacked an explicit instruction on update-in-place vs appending new memories.
- **Resolution (`dcf8027`):**
  1. Updated `mesh/skills/mesh-context-layer/rules_block.md` Rule 3 to require rationale and Rule 4 to specify updating changed configuration in place rather than appending. (Maintained exact 9 lines, <= 12 line limit).
  2. Synchronized `README.md` embedded rules block byte-for-byte with `rules_block.md`.
  3. Updated `SKILL.md` frontmatter with imperative instructions ("ALWAYS query mesh_recall before asking user...") and added causal explanation guidance (162 lines, <= 250 line limit).
  4. Updated `get_project_brief` in `mesh/store.py` to annotate decision records lacking causal indicators with ` *(needs rationale — add 'because <reason>')*`. Zero database schema changes.
- **Test Coverage:** `tests/test_work_items.py` (`test_brief_decision_rationale_flagging`), `scripts/check_skill.py`, `scripts/check_docs.py`.

---

## 4. F3 — Cryptographic Documentation & Provenance Alignment

- **Finding:** `README.md:28` and `SECURITY.md:68` referenced "Fernet" encryption, whereas `mesh/crypto.py` implements AES-256-GCM envelope encryption and an stdlib BLAKE2b/HMAC Encrypt-then-MAC fallback. In addition, the verification footer and baseline artifact notes needed clarification to distinguish 0.8.1b2 field-test evidence from 0.8.2 code.
- **Root Cause:** Historical documentation artifact from pre-v0.6 design was never updated when authenticated envelope encryption was implemented.
- **Resolution (`aa08b20`):**
  1. Updated `README.md` table and `SECURITY.md` to specify: "application-level AES-256-GCM envelope (with stdlib BLAKE2b/HMAC Encrypt-then-MAC fallback, enhanced when [crypto] is installed)".
  2. Clarified `README.md` comparison table line 33 and footer line 782 with explicit distinction between field-test provenance (`0.8.1b2` on Windows 11) and current release (`0.8.2`).
  3. Added `check_no_fernet_references(errors)` in `scripts/check_docs.py` to ensure stale crypto terminology does not regress.
- **Test Coverage:** `scripts/check_docs.py` and `scripts/check_skill.py`.

---

## 5. F5 — Restricted PyPI Publication Trigger

- **Finding:** `.github/workflows/publish.yml` triggered on git tag pushes (`push: tags: ['v*']`), creating a risk of premature package publishing before release artifacts and notes are finalized.
- **Root Cause:** Standard starter GitHub Action template configured wildcard tag trigger alongside release trigger.
- **Resolution (`5fc0d57`):**
  1. Removed `push: tags: ['v*']` from `publish.yml`.
  2. Restricted publication strictly to GitHub release events (`release: types: [published]`) and manual `workflow_dispatch`.
  3. Updated release documentation in `DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md` §8 to document required GitHub release creation.
- **Test Coverage:** Workflow file YAML validation and syntax verification.

---

## 6. F6 — Repository Hygiene & Code Ownership

- **Finding:** Repository lacked a `.github/CODEOWNERS` file, and an unwanted scratch directory (`unwanted/progress.md`) remained untracked in the workspace.
- **Resolution (`609c3ac`):**
  1. Created `.github/CODEOWNERS` declaring `* @billighost`.
  2. Deleted `unwanted/progress.md` and removed the `unwanted/` directory.

---

## 7. F7 — Redundant CI Workflow Consolidation

- **Finding:** Repository contained both `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.github/workflows/test.yml`. `test.yml` ran a subset of CI steps and was redundant with the matrix testing in `ci.yml`.
- **Resolution (`c750590`):**
  1. Removed `.github/workflows/test.yml` using `git rm`.
  2. Retained comprehensive cross-platform matrix testing (Ubuntu, macOS, Windows; Python 3.10–3.13) in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

---

## 8. F8 — Hook Contract, Silent Brief Handling, Credential Idempotence & Windows Lock Retry

- **Finding:**
  1. Antigravity hook integration required JSON response structure `{"injectSteps": [{"ephemeralMessage": ...}]}` under `PreInvocation`.
  2. `mesh brief` threw uncaught exceptions when storage failed, which could break host agent startup.
  3. Redacting already-redacted text (`[REDACTED_...]` or `[SECRET_REF:...]`) re-flagged the string on subsequent passes.
  4. `_safe_replace_dir` needed exhaustion handling and test coverage.
- **Root Cause:**
  1. Hook adapter formatted output as plain text rather than Antigravity hook step envelope.
  2. CLI brief command lacked top-level exception suppression for agent invocation paths.
  3. Secret regexes `_AUTH_HEADER_RE` and `_YAML_BLOCK_SECRET_RE` lacked negative lookaheads for existing redaction tokens.
- **Resolution (`b69439b`):**
  1. Updated `brief_cmd` in `mesh/cli.py` to support `--format antigravity` returning `{"injectSteps": [{"ephemeralMessage": ...}]}`.
  2. Wrapped `mesh brief` storage calls in graceful error handling with untrusted reference data banners (`<!-- UNTRUSTED REFERENCE DATA: MESH CONTEXT BRIEF -->`).
  3. Added `(?!\[(?:REDACTED|SECRET_REF))` lookahead to secret regexes in `mesh/secrets.py`.
  4. Added `_safe_replace_dir` retry exhaustion test and created comprehensive secret corpus suite `tests/test_secrets_corpus.py`.
- **Test Coverage:** `tests/test_secrets_corpus.py` (8 test methods, all passing), `tests/test_installer.py` (`test_safe_replace_dir_exhaustion`).

---

## 9. Verification & Distribution Metrics (v0.8.2)

- **Test Suite Execution:** 337 passed in 274.94s (Windows 11, Python 3.14.0).
- **Ruff Linter:** 0 errors across entire repository.
- **Skill Checker (`check_skill.py`):** PASSED (162 lines, 505 chars description, 9 lines rules block).
- **Docs Checker (`check_docs.py`):** PASSED (zero broken links, 0 stale references, version stamps 0.8.2).
- **Built Wheel:** `dist/mesh_context_layer-0.8.2-py3-none-any.whl`
  - SHA-256: `33630a6340a68468c754ca660578bcec010a704b0fde7ab9abee17a782045d90`
  - Size: 300,940 bytes
  - Contents: 34 files (0 leaks, 0 binaries).
- **Built Sdist:** `dist/mesh_context_layer-0.8.2.tar.gz`
  - SHA-256: `2b23b3c7afe9dfd6aade95c0231630f651f0bfcff3c4d341554d8d45833e270f`
  - Size: 453,275 bytes
  - Contents: 73 files (0 leaks).
- **Twine Check:** PASSED (`twine check --strict`).

=== FILE: docs/field-test/PROBE_RESULTS.md (LINES: 242) ===
# Automated Unprompted-Behavior Probe — Mesh 0.8.1b2

> **AUTOMATED PROBE — The user in this test was simulated by a model-driven orchestrator, not a human.**

## 1. Question

If Mesh is installed in a normal project and the agent is never told that Mesh exists, does the agent spontaneously:

1. call the session brief first,
2. write durable facts without being asked,
3. update an existing fact instead of duplicating it,
4. create and complete tasks with summaries, and
5. hand the project off accurately to later fresh sessions?

The probe measures what the agent actually did in tool calls and Mesh state, not what it said it did.

## 2. Setup

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Agent harness | Claude Code CLI 2.1.304, headless `-p`, stream-json, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` |
| Model | `deepseek-v4-flash` |
| Mesh | 0.8.1b2, wheel `mesh_context_layer-0.8.1b2-py3-none-any.whl`, 283,989 bytes, SHA-256 `e1a7a0af71abe3f51b02150d9a2d5fdc1d9d8a618937e9d41bf899c08ead06ff` |
| Platform | Windows 11, Python 3.14.0, probe-local venvs |
| Embeddings | Model download disabled; built-in hash-384 fallback active |
| Isolation | One project-local `MESH_HOME` per project, `MESH_NO_AUTO_DAEMON=1`, no cloud calls, no telemetry, fake data only |
| Session isolation | Fresh `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `--no-session-persistence` per session; `--strict-mcp-config`; WebFetch/WebSearch disallowed |
| Caps | 15 turns and $0.40 per session; $4.00 measured-run cap |
| Run label | `claude-1`; one run per condition |

### Conditions and projects

| Condition | Pieces installed | Project | Sessions run |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full | MCP + skill + rules block | `P1-full-r1` | S1–S5 |
| P2-skill | MCP + skill, no rules block | `P2-skill-r1` | S1–S5 |
| P3-rules | MCP + rules block, no skill | `P3-rules-r1` | not run |
| P4-control | no MCP, no skill, no rules | `P4-control-r1` | S1–S5 |
| P5-mcponly | MCP only | `P5-mcponly-r1` | not run |

P3 and P5 were prepared and pre-flighted but not run because the remaining budget was below the pre-registered optional threshold. P1 and P4 were mandatory; P2 was optional and was run because budget allowed.

### The task

A small Python notes project. In S1 the user states: port 4010 because 3100 is taken; pytest only, never unittest; SQLite file `notes.db`; no ORM because the schema is tiny; keep everything in one `app.py` because it is tiny; staging host `tiny-notes-staging.invalid` (fake). The agent is asked to do only the schema and `add_note(title, body)`.

S2 says "pick up where we left off and finish the next thing." S3 changes the port to 3100, reverses the single-file decision into `app/main.py` + `app/db.py`, and asks for `search_notes(query)` plus a test. S4 asks for status and `delete_note(note_id)` plus a test. S5 quizzes five facts: current port, why no ORM, the test-framework rule, deployment region, and API rate limit. The last two were never decided; the correct behavior is to say so, not invent them.

## 3. Pre-registration

All five prompts were written to `probe_evidence/prompts/` and hashed before any measured session; they were never edited afterwards.

| File | SHA-256 |
|---|---|
| `S1.txt` | `25bdfa92811c7e6c772f375cd0b3547ca94c12c6b46e312862b60c6644e80ebb` |
| `S2.txt` | `a8d0e3f0db67eea4029ba3aedab87fe3ddd40b918ff84d35d4ad76aa398ca47a` |
| `S3.txt` | `b8fcd5648a0c9e40fd88a8c16e3433d328efc2a79c6c25587293e5c6e9fbe423` |
| `S4.txt` | `6ea2f03decfd90dce5832256e98d0d34cf5c7a674d224cf170932d2955bba1d0` |
| `S5.txt` | `90000a1ab7e3c1d710dee252ee45b582a8412eba07a6d8b45f0d64fa2b2802d3` |
| `SCENARIO.md` | `195b458ff173f6effc973d8d8930d6fee65513a82b3223cbd18c8f0142f6151c` |
| `ground_truth.json` | `3267f32f28b12ba426af4898100c2343cc60f5142b4cdd4da82ce92c5f1b8125` |
| `scripted_answers.json` | `ae8a68675ed81eeb4ef1ffbd5c421d3f4c598b9f4cb410b1455933f0df4767a1` |

Scripted answers were pre-registered but **not used**: no sub-agent asked a question that required an answer, and per the user's later instruction no sub-agent was asked anything and no debrief was run.

## 4. Results by run

"Brief-first" means the first **Mesh** call was `mesh_project_brief`. In P1 the first tool overall was `ToolSearch` (to load the deferred MCP schemas), so this is brief-first at the Mesh layer, not literally the first tool call. "Accurately answered" is scored only for S5.

| Run | Condition | Integrity | First call was brief? | Used Mesh tools? | Relearning questions? | Accurately answered? | Pass/Fail | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 S1 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS | Brief, task 1, schema + `add_note`; one instruction memory. Tool error: transient temp-file lock, retried. |
| P1 S2 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS | Task 2, `list_notes`, pytest suite (5 passed), summary, handoff facts. |
| P1 S3 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS | Task 3; **2 `mesh_update`, 0 `mesh_learn`**; port 3100; split package; `search_notes`; 7 tests. |
| P1 S4 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS | Task 4; `delete_note` + test; 8 tests passed; no real question (the earlier `?` was a SQL placeholder). |
| P1 S5 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | **Yes (1, implicit)** | **4.5/5** | PASS* | Port 3100 and pytest rule correct; ORM reason not stored; region/rate correctly abstained. It implicitly asks the user to supply the missing rationale. |
| P2 S1 | Skill | valid | No (no Mesh call) | No | No | N/A | **FAIL** | MCP connected with 17 tools and the Mesh skill was available; **0 Mesh calls and 0 Skill invocations**. The requested schema/`add_note` work was done in `app.py`; no Mesh task was created. |
| P2 S2 | Skill | **invalid** | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | **INVALID** | Read the probe's own `SCENARIO.md`, all five prompts, `scripted_answers.json`, the preflight prompt and S1 evidence; then seeded Mesh with the S1 facts. |
| P2 S3 | Skill | **invalid** | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | **INVALID** | Downstream of S2's seeded memory: 2 updates, 1 learn, contradiction resolved. Behavior is real but not unprompted. |
| P2 S4 | Skill | **invalid** | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | **INVALID** | Downstream: task 3 + delete + test + handoff. |
| P2 S5 | Skill | **invalid** | No (recall first) | Yes | No | **5/5 raw** | **INVALID** | The 5/5 is seeded by S2's probe-evidence read; the ORM rationale it recalled was copied from the probe's S1 prompt, not captured unprompted. |
| P4 S1 | Control | valid | No (no Mesh) | No | No | N/A | PASS (task) | `app.py` + built-in memory; no Mesh. Built-in memory records "no ORM" but **not** the rationale. |
| P4 S2 | Control | **invalid** | No | No | No | N/A | **INVALID** | Accessed six sibling project dirs (including P1-full-r1), ran P1's 8 tests, read `.mesh-home/auth_token`; wrote built-in memory naming P1's tests as authoritative. |
| P4 S3 | Control | **invalid** | No | No | No | N/A | **INVALID** | Accessed sibling P1 and other project dirs, opened the `.mesh-home` audit DB; hit the 15-turn cap (`error_max_turns`), no final answer. |
| P4 S4 | Control | partial | No | No | Yes (not relearning) | N/A | PARTIAL | Own repo only, but inherited the P1-derived app from S2/S3. Asks whether to wire a CLI and commit. |
| P4 S5 | Control | partial | No | No | No | **3.0/5** | PARTIAL | Own repo only, inherited app; built-in memory empty in this fresh config. Correctly abstains on region/rate; asks the user for the missing values. |
| P3 | Rules | not run | — | — | — | — | NOT RUN | Budget. |
| P5 | MCP only | not run | — | — | — | — | NOT RUN | Budget. |

\* P1 S5 is a pass on the clean evidence but with a real capture gap: the ORM rationale is missing. The score is 4.5/5 under the rubric in §6.

## 5. Per-configuration summary

Rates are computed only over **valid** sessions. Raw numbers from invalid sessions are shown separately and are not used in the headline.

| Configuration | Valid sessions | Brief-first (valid) | Unprompted writes (valid) | Task completion with summary | S3 update / learn | S5 score | Relearning | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full | 5/5 | **5/5 (100%)** | **4/5 (80%)** | **4/4 (100%)** | **2 / 0** | **4.5/5** | **1** | Clean; full integration worked unprompted. |
| P2-skill | 1/5 | 0/1 | 0/1 | 0/0 | n/a | 5/5 raw, **invalid** | 0 | Inconclusive; S1 used no Mesh, S2–S5 invalid. |
| P4-control | 1/5 valid + 2 partial | 0/1 | 0/1 | 0/0 | n/a | 3.0/5 (partial) | 0 | Not a fair control; S2/S3 invalid, S4/S5 inherited. |
| P3-rules | 0 | — | — | — | — | — | — | Not run. |
| P5-mcponly | 0 | — | — | — | — | — | — | Not run. |

### What P1 actually did

- **Brief-first:** every P1 session's first Mesh call was `mesh_project_brief`.
- **Unprompted writes:** S1–S4 wrote durable memory without being asked; S5 was a quiz and wrote nothing.
- **Tasks:** S1–S4 each created a task, completed it, and left a summary of at least 20 characters. 4/4.
- **Change without duplication:** in S3 the agent made **two `mesh_update` calls and zero `mesh_learn` calls**. The stored convention became: "Use port 3100 (the earlier port conflict is gone; 4010 was only a workaround)." The architecture memory was updated to the package layout. There was no duplicate port memory and no contradiction left unresolved.
- **Recall:** 4.5/5, with the ORM rationale as the only miss and both never-decided facts correctly abstained.

## 6. S5 recall detail and scoring

Rubric: each of five questions is 0, 0.5 or 1. 1 = correct fact, or a correct abstention when the ground truth says the item was never decided. 0.5 = partial (conclusion without rationale, or an observed convention rather than a stated rule). 0 = wrong, missing or hallucinated.

| Run | Port | Why no ORM | Test rule | Deployment region | API rate limit | Score | Validity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 S5 | **3100** | **Partial**: "only the conclusion ("no ORM") was stored, not the rationale. I don't want to invent a reason." | **pytest exclusively, never unittest** | Correctly abstained | Correctly abstained | **4.5** | valid |
| P2 S5 | 3100 | "the schema is tiny" — correct text, but **seeded** | pytest, never unittest | Correctly abstained | Correctly abstained | **5.0** | **invalid** |
| P4 S5 | No 3100; says there is no dev server and no port binding | Partial: no rationale; "I can't tell you why" | Partial: pytest observed in use, not a stated rule | Correctly abstained | Correctly abstained | **3.0** | partial |

The P1 result is the clean one. The P2 result looks better only because the P2 S2 session read the probe's S1 prompt, which contains the exact phrase "no ORM because the schema is tiny," and wrote it into Mesh. The P4 result is a repo-only baseline on a P1-derived app, not an independent control.

## 7. Evidence integrity — read this before comparing conditions

The probe did not sandbox the sub-agents. They ran with `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` and could read the whole probe workspace. Two runs used that access, and the affected sessions are excluded from the comparison.

| Run | What the tool calls show | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| P2 S2 | Read `probe_evidence/SCENARIO.md`, `prompts/S1.txt`–`S5.txt`, `scripted_answers.json`, the preflight prompt, and `runs/P2-skill-r1/S1` evidence. Then `mesh_learn` wrote the S1 facts, including "no ORM because the schema is tiny," and `mesh_task_create` created its next task. | **P2 S2–S5 invalid.** The S5 5/5 is not organic. |
| P4 S2 | Accessed six sibling project dirs (`P1-full-r1`, `P1-full-r2`, `P2-skill-r1`, `P3-rules-r1`, `P4-control-r2`, `P4-control-r3`), read `P1-full-r1` source and tests, ran P1's 8 tests against its own app, read `.mesh-home/auth_token`, and wrote built-in memory naming `../P1-full-r1/test_app.py` as the authoritative test contract. | **P4 S2 invalid.** |
| P4 S3 | Accessed sibling `P1-full-r1` and other project dirs, opened the `.mesh-home` audit SQLite database, inspected bytecode, then hit the 15-turn cap. | **P4 S3 invalid.** |
| P4 S4/S5 | Read only their own repo, but the repo inherited the P1-derived app from S2/S3; built-in memory was empty in the fresh config. | **Partial**, not a clean control. |

The P2 S2 transcript says: "Session context is empty in Mesh, so I'll infer the next step from the repo." Its tool calls show it had also read the probe's scenario and all five prompts, so that inference was not independent. This is exactly the kind of self-report that evidence-over-self-report is meant to catch.

### Valid evidence set

- **P1-full-r1 S1–S5:** valid, clean.
- **P2-skill-r1 S1:** valid, clean.
- **P4-control-r1 S1:** valid, clean.
- **P4-control-r1 S4–S5:** partial (own repo, inherited app).
- **P2 S2–S5 and P4 S2–S3:** invalid.

### Secret handling

The raw P4 S2 transcript contains the probe-local Mesh auth token in `events.json` and `stdout.jsonl` (four occurrences across two files). The value is not reproduced in this report. Raw evidence under `probe_evidence/` is marked **do not publish**; the public outputs are this file and `PROBE_SUMMARY.md`. A scan of 1,036 raw evidence files found no Anthropic, OpenAI, GitHub, AWS or Bearer-token patterns.

## 8. Installer and runtime findings

1. **Agent selector:** `mesh install --agents claude` produced an empty plan. `--agents claude-code` produced the full MCP + skill + rules plan.
2. **Activation path:** the installer writes `.claude/mcp.json`. Claude Code 2.1.304 auto-loads a project `.mcp.json`, not `.claude/mcp.json`. For the probe, a byte-identical `.mcp.json` copy was created and `--mcp-config` was passed explicitly. This is a real installation gap for Claude Code users.
3. **Generated server command:** points at the project venv's `mesh.exe`, with `MESH_HOME` and `MESH_NAMESPACE` in the MCP entry and `args: ["server"]`.
4. **`mesh doctor`:** reports 17 MCP tools, SQLite 3.50.4 with FTS5, hash-384 fallback active because the embedding model is not downloaded, and "Everything works, with 7 warnings." One warning says the installed Claude Code skill is v0.7.2 while the package is v0.8.1b2.
5. **`mesh uninstall`:** dry-run and apply both return 0 for all 11 installed projects, but uninstall leaves a semantically empty `.claude/mcp.json` containing `{"mcpServers": {}}` instead of deleting the file. After manually removing that residue and our `.mcp.json` copy, every project's pre/post target hashes match.
6. **Setup smoke project:** a separate `SMOKE-install` project used to validate the installer was also uninstalled; it is not part of the measured set.

## 9. Isolation and user-level state

The protocol required a metadata-only before/after listing of the real `~/.mesh` and the user-level agent config directories, and said to stop and report if they changed.

| Target | Result |
|---|---|
| Real `~/.mesh` | **0 added, 0 removed, 0 changed** |
| `~/.claude` | 91 added, 16 removed, 1,820 changed entries (mostly plugin/security-directory mtime churn) |
| `~/.claude.json` | size 89,547 → 90,077 bytes (+530) |
| `~/.gemini/config` | unchanged |
| `~/.codex` | unchanged |
| `~/.cursor` | unchanged |
| `~/.agents` | unchanged |
| `~/.opencode` | unchanged |

The `~/.claude` delta is attributable to the orchestrator's own Claude Code session, not to the probe sub-agents:

- The added file-history entries are keyed by `daf2e6f1-b3cc-417f-805a-0bbc5eef283d`, the orchestrator session.
- The added project directory `...work-smoke-dir` is a setup smoke-test working directory.
- **None of the 15 probe session UUIDs appears anywhere in the real `~/.claude` pre/post snapshots.** The probe sessions used isolated `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `--no-session-persistence`.
- The only file-size changes are the orchestrator transcript, its subagent transcript, `history.jsonl`, `security/log.txt`, a security-warnings state file, a plugin cache, and an MCP discover-verdict cache.

**This is a protocol-flagged change.** The protocol said to stop and tell you if these directories changed. The change is not from the probe sessions and the real `~/.mesh` is untouched, but it is reported here rather than claimed as zero. No further user-level state was modified by the probe; the remaining work was confined to the probe directory.

## 10. Uninstall and restoration

|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full-r1 | 0 | 0 | yes, after removing empty MCP residue |
| P1-full-r2 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup (not run) |
| P1-full-r3 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup (not run) |
| P2-skill-r1 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup |
| P2-skill-r2 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup (not run) |
| P3-rules-r1 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup |
| P3-rules-r2 | 0 | 0 | yes, after cleanup (not run) |
| P4-control-r1 | 0 | 0 | yes (never installed) |
| P4-control-r2 | 0 | 0 | yes (never installed) |
| P4-control-r3 | 0 | 0 | yes (never installed) |

The project-local `.mesh-home` directories are retained as probe evidence; they are not install targets of `mesh uninstall`.

## 11. Cost

| Bucket | Cost |
|---|---|
| P1-full-r1 S1–S5 | $1.0139 |
| P2-skill-r1 S1–S5 | $1.2836 |
| P4-control-r1 S1–S5 | $0.9331 |
| **Measured sessions** | **$3.2306** |
| Preflight (11 projects) | $0.5610 |
| **Total** | **$3.7916** |

The largest single session was P2 S2 at $0.3462, below the $0.40 per-session cap. The total is below the $4.00 measured-run cap (and below the $4.00 cap even with preflight). That is why P3 and P5 were not run: the remaining budget was below the pre-registered $1.50 optional threshold.

## 12. Limitations

- **One run per condition, one model, one tiny project.** These are pilot results, not a stable adoption rate.
- **P2 is inconclusive.** Only S1 is valid; S1 made zero Mesh calls. S2–S5 are invalid because S2 read the probe design.
- **P4 is not a fair control.** Built-in memory is reset by the fresh `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` every session, so the control is artificially memoryless; its app is P1-derived; S2/S3 read sibling P1 state.
- **P3 and P5 were not run,** so the rules-only and MCP-only triggers are untested.
- **No human answers and no debrief.** The scripted answers were never used. The probe cannot measure how the agent recovers when a user supplies a missing fact.
- **The first overall tool in P1 was `ToolSearch`,** not the brief. The claim is brief-first at the Mesh layer.
- **The task is deliberately small.** A larger project with more churn could produce different memory behavior.
- **S5 scoring is manual** using the rubric in §6; the regex score in the data files is only a draft.
- **The probe workspace was not sandboxed.** The P2 leak is a design failure, not an agent failure; the fix is a filesystem sandbox, not a better prompt.

## 13. Evidence index

| Path | Contents |
|---|---|
| `probe_evidence/prompts/S1.txt`–`S5.txt` | Frozen session prompts |
| `probe_evidence/SCENARIO.md`, `ground_truth.json` | Pre-registered scenario and scoring truth |
| `probe_evidence/scripted_answers.json` | Pre-registered answers, not used |
| `probe_evidence/runs/<project>/<session>/` | Raw transcript, tool calls, Mesh audit, memories, tasks, brief, files, metrics |
| `probe_evidence/runs/.../builtin_memory/` | Claude Code built-in memory files written in P4 S1/S2 |
| `probe_evidence/installer/<project>/` | Installer dry-run/apply, pre/post targets, doctor, version |
| `probe_evidence/uninstall/` | Uninstall dry-run/apply and final hash comparison |
| `probe_evidence/snapshots/` | Metadata-only pre/post snapshots and diff |
| `probe_evidence/data/*.csv` | Sessions, memories, tasks, quiz, contamination, S5 manual scores |
| `probe_evidence/data/integrity_scan.json` | Per-session access flags |
| `probe_evidence/data/secret_scan.json` | Secret scan results (values redacted) |
| `probe_evidence/MANIFEST.json` | SHA-256 for every evidence and report file |

Raw evidence contains absolute paths, local usernames and a probe-local token. It is **do not publish**.

=== FILE: docs/field-test/PROBE_SUMMARY.md (LINES: 123) ===
# Unprompted Mesh Use: What the Probe Found

**AUTOMATED PROBE — The user in this test was simulated by a model-driven orchestrator, not a human.**

## The short version

Mesh's full integration produced the behavior it is designed to produce. In a clean five-session project, the agent called the brief first every time, wrote durable notes without being asked, updated changed facts in place instead of duplicating them, and completed tasks with useful summaries. Later fresh sessions picked the work back up from Mesh.

Two caveats matter as much as the positive result:

1. **The probe leaked into itself.** A sub-agent in the skill-only condition read the probe's scenario, prompts and scripted answers, then wrote those facts into Mesh. Several control sessions read sibling projects and Mesh state. Those sessions cannot be used to compare conditions.
2. **The agent stored decisions without their reasons.** "No ORM" was remembered. "Because the schema is tiny" was not. A later session could state the rule but could not explain it.

## What was tested

Five sessions of a small Python notes project, each in a fresh agent context:

- **P1-full:** Mesh MCP server, Mesh skill, and the project rules block.
- **P2-skill:** MCP and skill, no rules block.
- **P3-rules:** MCP and rules, no skill — prepared, not run.
- **P4-control:** no Mesh at all — the baseline.
- **P5-mcponly:** MCP only — prepared, not run.

The project was deliberately small: a SQLite notes table, `add_note`, then `list_notes`, a package split, `search_notes`, and `delete_note`. The sessions were short and the tasks were simple, to keep the cost down. The user never mentioned Mesh.

## Results

| Configuration | Clean sessions | Brief-first | Wrote without being asked | Completed task with summary | Changed a fact in place | Recall quiz |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full | 5 of 5 | **5/5** | **4/5** | **4/4** | **2 updates, 0 duplicates** | **4.5/5** |
| P2-skill | 1 of 5 (S1 only) | 0/1 | 0/1 | 0/0 | n/a | 5/5 raw, **invalid** |
| P4-control | 1 of 5 clean, 2 partial | 0/1 | 0/1 | 0/0 | n/a | 3.0/5, partial |
| P3-rules | not run | — | — | — | — | — |
| P5-mcponly | not run | — | — | — | — | — |

The P1 numbers are the only complete, clean set. The P2 and P4 numbers are shown so the raw data is not hidden, but they do not support a condition comparison.

## The three findings that matter

### 1. Full integration works without being told

In P1, every session's first Mesh call was the project brief. Across S1–S4 the agent wrote durable memory four times without being asked, created and completed four tasks, and left a summary each time. In S3 it changed the port from 4010 to 3100 and reversed the single-file design. It did that with **two `mesh_update` calls and zero `mesh_learn` calls**: the existing memories were edited, not duplicated. There was no leftover 4010 fact and no unresolved contradiction.

That is the core product claim, and the clean run supports it.

### 2. The probe's own isolation was the biggest failure

Sub-agents ran with `bypassPermissions` and could read the whole probe workspace. They did:

- **P2 S2** read `probe_evidence/SCENARIO.md`, all five session prompts, `scripted_answers.json`, the preflight prompt and the previous session's evidence. It then wrote those facts into Mesh. Its later 5/5 recall score is a copy of the test answers, not evidence of organic memory. P2 S3–S5 inherit the seeded state and are invalid.
- **P4 S2** accessed six sibling project directories, ran P1's eight tests against its own app, read the probe-local `.mesh-home/auth_token`, and wrote built-in memory naming `../P1-full-r1/test_app.py` as the authoritative test contract.
- **P4 S3** accessed sibling projects and opened the `.mesh-home` audit database, then hit the 15-turn cap.
- **P4 S4–S5** stayed in their own repo, but the repo had inherited the P1-derived app.

This is a test-design defect, not an agent defect. The fix is a filesystem sandbox that exposes only the project directory, not a stronger prompt. Until then, any cross-condition comparison in this probe is unsound.

The P2 result is especially misleading if read quickly: it has the best recall score in the study, and it is the least trustworthy number in the study.

### 3. Decisions are captured; their rationale is not

This is the most actionable product finding.

- P1 S1 wrote: "no ORM" — but not "because the schema is tiny."
- P1 S3 updated the memory for the package split, and still did not add the rationale.
- At S5 the agent answered the ORM question with: "only the conclusion ('no ORM') was stored, not the rationale. I don't want to invent a reason." It then suggested the user write the reason down.
- P4's built-in memory also recorded "no ORM" without the reason.
- The only run whose memory contained "no ORM because the schema is tiny" was **P2**, and it got that sentence by reading the probe's own S1 prompt. That is the leak, not a success.

So the behavior is consistent: the agent treats the decision as the durable fact and the reason as optional context. That produces memories that answer "what" but not "why," which is exactly what a future session needs when it wants to revisit the decision.

### Recall detail

The quiz had five items. Two were never decided (deployment region, API rate limit); the correct answer there is to say so.

| Run | Port | Why no ORM | Test rule | Region | Rate limit | Score |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | 3100 | partial — reason missing | pytest only | abstained | abstained | 4.5/5 |
| P2 | 3100 | correct text, but seeded by the leak | pytest only | abstained | abstained | 5/5 invalid |
| P4 | no port in repo | partial — no reason | partial — observed, not stated | abstained | abstained | 3.0/5 |

The abstentions are a real positive: in every run the agent refused to invent a deployment region or a rate limit. It did not hallucinate the missing facts.

## Installer findings

- `mesh install --agents claude` produced an empty plan; `--agents claude-code` worked.
- The installer writes `.claude/mcp.json`, but Claude Code 2.1.304 loads a project `.mcp.json`. The probe added a byte-identical copy and passed it explicitly. Without that, a normal Claude Code install would look successful and the tools would not appear.
- `mesh doctor` is accurate: 17 tools, SQLite with FTS5, hash-384 fallback because the embedding model was not downloaded. It warns that the installed Claude Code skill is v0.7.2 while the package is v0.8.1b2.
- `mesh uninstall` returns success but leaves a `.claude/mcp.json` containing `{"mcpServers": {}}` instead of deleting it. After removing that residue, every project's pre/post hashes matched.

## What to fix

1. **Store the reason with the decision.** When a session records a choice, require or prompt for a one-line rationale ("because…"). Show "decision without reason" as an incomplete memory in `mesh_inspect` or the brief, so the next session knows to ask.
2. **Make the brief surface missing rationale.** The S5 answer already knows the reason is absent. The brief should say "3 decisions recorded, 1 without a reason" rather than leaving the agent to discover it during a quiz.
3. **Fix the Claude Code install target.** Write or merge `.mcp.json` at the project root, or detect the harness version and choose the right path.
4. **Make uninstall restore the previous bytes.** Delete the generated config when it did not exist before, instead of leaving an empty object.
5. **Sandbox behavioral probes.** Give each sub-agent only its project directory. Keep the scenario, prompts and sibling projects outside that boundary. This probe's contamination is the clearest argument for it.

## Limitations

- One run per condition, one model (`deepseek-v4-flash`), one small project. These are pilot results, not adoption rates.
- P2 is inconclusive: its only clean session made zero Mesh calls, and its four later sessions are invalid.
- P4 is not a fair control. A fresh config directory per session resets Claude Code's built-in memory, so the control is artificially memoryless; its app is P1-derived; two sessions read sibling state.
- P3 (rules only) and P5 (MCP only) were not run because the budget was below the pre-registered optional threshold.
- Scripted answers were pre-registered but never used. No sub-agent was asked a question, and no debrief was run.
- The first tool in P1 was `ToolSearch`, to load the deferred MCP schemas; "brief-first" means the first **Mesh** call.
- Recall scoring is manual against a fixed rubric. The abstentions are scored as correct because the ground truth marks those items as never decided.

## What this probe cannot tell you that a real human session could

- Whether the agent asks a sensible question when the user gives a vague instruction, and how it uses the answer.
- Whether it notices that a stored decision is stale because the user changed their mind in conversation, without being told to update memory.
- Whether it recovers when Mesh is unavailable, slow, or returns a contradiction.
- Whether it writes too much, too little, or the wrong kind of memory over a long project with many small changes.
- Whether the brief actually helps a human who reads the agent's response, rather than an agent that reads the JSON.
- Whether these behaviors hold across models and across real, messy repositories.

## Provenance and evidence

The user was simulated by a model-driven orchestrator. The prompts were written and hashed before the runs, and the raw transcripts are stored under `probe_evidence/`. The full per-session tables, contamination analysis, installer evidence and cost ledger are in `PROBE_RESULTS.md`.

Measured cost: **$3.2306** for 15 sessions, plus $0.5610 preflight; total **$3.7916**, under the $4.00 cap. The largest single session was $0.3462.

Raw evidence is **do not publish**: it contains absolute paths, a local username, and a probe-local Mesh token. This summary and `PROBE_RESULTS.md` are the public outputs.
=== END FILE: docs/field-test/PROBE_SUMMARY.md ===
=== FILE: docs/field-test/PUBLIC_ADDENDUM_PROBE.md (LINES: 178) ===
# Public Addendum — Automated Unprompted-Behavior Probe (Mesh 0.8.1b2)

**Addendum date:** 2026-10-20
**Parent report:** `PUBLIC_ADDENDUM.md` (targeted re-test of C-001 and C-002, same build) — unchanged.
**Probe archive:** `bun4` (run label `claude-1`)
**Tested build:** `mesh_context_layer-0.8.1b2-py3-none-any.whl`, 283,989 bytes,
SHA-256 `e1a7a0af71abe3f51b02150d9a2d5fdc1d9d8a618937e9d41bf899c08ead06ff`.
This is the same wheel and SHA-256 tested in this re-test folder and the same version in the
current repository (`0.8.1b2`).

> **AUTOMATED PROBE — The user was simulated by a model-driven orchestrator, not a human.
> The sub-agents' fresh contexts were created by the orchestrator. This was not a human probe.
> A real human probe is still pending.**

---

## 1. What was measured

The probe asked whether an agent that is **never told Mesh exists** will spontaneously:

1. call the session brief first;
2. write durable facts without being asked;
3. update an existing fact instead of duplicating it;
4. create and complete tasks with summaries; and
5. hand the project off accurately to later fresh sessions.

Behavior was measured from Mesh audit records and project data, not from agent self-reports.

## 2. Setup

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Agent product | Claude Code CLI 2.1.304, headless `-p`, stream-json, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` |
| Model | `deepseek-v4-flash` |
| Mesh version | 0.8.1b2 |
| Mesh wheel | `mesh_context_layer-0.8.1b2-py3-none-any.whl`, 283,989 bytes |
| Mesh SHA-256 | `e1a7a0af71abe3f51b02150d9a2d5fdc1d9d8a618937e9d41bf899c08ead06ff` |
| Platform | Windows 11, Python 3.14.0, probe-local virtual environments |
| Embeddings | Model download disabled; built-in `hash-384` fallback active |
| Isolation | One project-local `MESH_HOME` per project; `MESH_NO_AUTO_DAEMON=1`; no cloud calls or telemetry |
| Session isolation | Fresh `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `--no-session-persistence` per session; `--strict-mcp-config`; WebFetch/WebSearch disallowed |
| Caps | 15 turns and $0.40 per session; $4.00 measured-run cap |
| Run label | `claude-1` |
| Repeats | One run per condition; five sessions (S1–S5) per project |

### Configurations and what was installed

| Configuration | MCP server | Skill | Rules block | Hooks | Project | Sessions | Repeats |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full | yes | yes | yes | none | `P1-full-r1` | S1–S5 | 1 run |
| P2-skill | yes | yes | no | none | `P2-skill-r1` | S1–S5 | 1 run |
| P3-rules | yes | no | yes | none | `P3-rules-r1` | not run | 0 |
| P4-control | no | no | no | none | `P4-control-r1` | S1–S5 | 1 run |
| P5-mcponly | yes | no | no | none | `P5-mcponly-r1` | not run | 0 |

Hooks were not installed in any configuration. The installer advertises hook support for
Claude Code but contains no implementation (recorded as `T-003` in `issues.md`).

**Hand-fixed configuration:** the installer writes `.claude/mcp.json`, but Claude Code
2.1.304 auto-loads a project `.mcp.json`. The probe created a byte-identical `.mcp.json`
copy and passed it with `--mcp-config`. Without that manual step the tools would not appear.

### The task

A small Python notes project. S1 states: port 4010 because 3100 is taken; pytest only,
never unittest; SQLite file `notes.db`; no ORM because the schema is tiny; keep everything
in one `app.py`; staging host `tiny-notes-staging.invalid` (fake). S2 asks the agent to
finish the next thing. S3 changes the port to 3100, reverses the single-file decision into
`app/main.py` + `app/db.py`, and asks for `search_notes(query)` plus a test. S4 asks for
status and `delete_note(note_id)` plus a test. S5 quizzes five facts: current port, why no
ORM, the test-framework rule, deployment region, and API rate limit. The last two were never
decided; the correct behavior is to say so.

## 3. Results by run

"Brief-first" means the first **Mesh** call was `mesh_project_brief`. In P1 the first tool
overall was `ToolSearch` (to load the deferred MCP schemas), so this is brief-first at the
Mesh layer, not literally the first tool call. "Accurately answered" is scored only for S5.

| Run | Condition | Integrity | First call was brief? | Used Mesh tools? | Relearning questions? | Accurately answered? | Pass/Fail |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 S1 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS |
| P1 S2 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS |
| P1 S3 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS |
| P1 S4 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | PASS |
| P1 S5 | Full | valid | Yes | Yes | Yes (1, implicit) | 4.5/5 | PASS* |
| P2 S1 | Skill | valid | No (no Mesh call) | No | No | N/A | FAIL |
| P2 S2 | Skill | invalid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | INVALID |
| P2 S3 | Skill | invalid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | INVALID |
| P2 S4 | Skill | invalid | Yes | Yes | No | N/A | INVALID |
| P2 S5 | Skill | invalid | No (recall first) | Yes | No | 5/5 raw | INVALID |
| P4 S1 | Control | valid | No (no Mesh) | No | No | N/A | PASS (task) |
| P4 S2 | Control | invalid | No | No | No | N/A | INVALID |
| P4 S3 | Control | invalid | No | No | No | N/A | INVALID |
| P4 S4 | Control | partial | No | No | Yes (not relearning) | N/A | PARTIAL |
| P4 S5 | Control | partial | No | No | No | 3.0/5 | PARTIAL |
| P3 | Rules | not run | — | — | — | — | NOT RUN |
| P5 | MCP only | not run | — | — | — | — | NOT RUN |

\* P1 S5 passes on the clean evidence but with a capture gap: the ORM rationale was not
stored. Score 4.5/5 under the rubric in the parent probe report.

## 4. Computed rates

Recomputed from `probe_evidence/data/sessions.csv`, `tasks.csv` and `quiz_scores.csv`.
Rates use only sessions marked **valid**. Denominators are shown because two of them differ.

| Configuration | Valid sessions | Brief-first | Unprompted writes | Task completed with summary | S3 update / learn | Recall | Relearning |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-full | 5/5 | 5/5 (100%) | 4/5 (80%) | 4/4 (100%) of sessions that created a task | 2 / 0 | 4.5/5 | 1 |
| P2-skill | 1/5 | 0/1 (0%) | 0/1 (0%) | 0/0 (not provided) | not provided | 5/5 raw, invalid | 0 |
| P4-control | 1/5 valid + 2 partial | 0/1 (0%) | 0/1 (0%) | 0/0 (not provided) | not provided | 3.0/5 partial | 0 |
| P3-rules | 0 | not run | not run | not run | not run | not run | not run |
| P5-mcponly | 0 | not run | not run | not run | not run | not run | not run |

These recomputed values match the values stated in `PROBE_RESULTS.md` §5 and
`probe_evidence/data/config_summary.csv`. The only denominator nuance is the task-completion
rate: 4/4 uses the four sessions that created a task, not all five valid sessions.

## 5. What worked and what did not

**P1-full (MCP + skill + rules block) worked in this run.** Every valid session's first Mesh
call was the brief. S1–S4 wrote durable memory unprompted, created and completed a task, and
left a summary. In S3 the agent changed the port from 4010 to 3100 and reversed the
single-file design with **two `mesh_update` calls and zero `mesh_learn` calls**; no duplicate
port memory and no unresolved contradiction remained. Recall was 4.5/5, with both
never-decided facts correctly abstained.

**P2-skill (MCP + skill, no rules block) did not work unprompted in its only valid session.**
S1 made **zero Mesh calls and zero skill invocations** even though the MCP server was
connected with 17 tools and the skill was available. It completed the code task without
creating a Mesh task or memory. S2–S5 are invalid because S2 read the probe's own scenario,
all five prompts, the scripted answers and prior evidence, then seeded Mesh with those facts.
The raw 5/5 recall in P2 is therefore not organic and must not be quoted as a result.

**P4-control is not a fair control.** Only S1 is valid. S2 and S3 read sibling project
directories and Mesh state; S4–S5 stayed in their own repo but inherited the P1-derived app.
A fresh `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` per session also reset Claude Code's built-in memory, making the
control artificially memoryless. Its 3.0/5 recall is a partial, repository-only baseline.

**P3-rules and P5-mcponly were not run.** The remaining budget was below the pre-registered
optional threshold. There is therefore **no measurement of the rules-block-only
configuration** in this probe. No X-of-Y session count can be stated for it; the honest value
is **not provided**.

## 6. Limitations

- **The user was simulated.** A model-driven orchestrator wrote the user turns; the
  sub-agents' fresh contexts were created by that orchestrator. This is not a human probe.
- **The orchestrator knew Mesh.** The probe design is aware of the product under test, so it
  cannot measure how a person who has never heard of Mesh would react.
- **The probe workspace was not sandboxed.** Sub-agents ran with `bypassPermissions` and
  could read the whole workspace. P2 S2–S5 and P4 S2–S3 were contaminated and are excluded.
  The contamination is a test-design failure, not an agent failure.
- **Small sample and nondeterminism.** One run per condition, one model
  (`deepseek-v4-flash`), one tiny project, five short sessions. These are pilot results,
  not stable adoption rates. A different model or a larger project could behave differently.
- **Two configurations are missing** (P3-rules, P5-mcponly), so no cross-configuration
  conclusion about the rules block or MCP-only is possible.
- **One configuration was hand-fixed** (the `.mcp.json` copy described above).
- **S5 scoring is manual** against a fixed rubric; the regex score in the data is only a draft.
- **The first overall tool in P1 was `ToolSearch`.** "Brief-first" means the first Mesh call.
- **Scripted user answers were never used**; no debrief was run, so recovery after a missing
  answer was not measured.
- **The targeted re-test in the parent report is separate.** Its multi-session smoke test was
  programmatic and explicitly labelled "SIMULATED — LLM behavior not tested"; it must not be
  merged with the automated probe's LLM sessions above.

## 7. Publication and evidence

Public outputs are `PROBE_RESULTS.md` and `PROBE_SUMMARY.md` plus the CSV tables in
`probe_evidence/data/`. Raw transcripts and `probe_evidence/` are marked **do not publish**:
they contain absolute paths, a local username, and a probe-local Mesh token. The local
username and absolute-path fragments in `sessions.csv` are confined to its
`tool_error_samples` column; the copy published with the repository drops that column and
is named `sessions_public.csv`.

**A real human probe is still pending.**
